What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… I keep hearing someone’s voice. Garrett’s. It sounds like he’s right behind me sometimes—on a trail, or when I’m in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Is Garrett's voice you hear saying anything to you, or is it just a sound?\n\nPatient: It’s words. His voice. Not just noise.\n\nSometimes it’s like he’s right behind me, saying something clear enough that I turn around… even though I know he’s gone. In the moment, it doesn’t feel like a memory. It feels real.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: Not exactly… I mean, it’s his voice, clear, like he’s right behind me. On the trail, or in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens it feels real. I don’t really want to get into all of what he says.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice influence your actions or decisions in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe. When I hear it, it feels like he’s right there behind me, and I’ll stop what I’m doing or turn around. Part of me knows Garrett’s dead, but in that moment it feels real enough that I react.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett go away when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No. Not really.\n\nI can try to ignore it, or leave the room, but when it happens it’s just… there. Like Garrett’s standing behind me. It feels too real in the moment to just make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Is Garrett's voice ever critical of you or telling you that you're doing something wrong?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels that way, yeah. I don’t like talking about it much. It’s Garrett’s voice, clear… like he’s right behind me. Even when I know he’s gone, in that moment it feels real, and it can make me feel like I’m doing something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett happen when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No… I can’t make it happen. It just comes on its own. Sometimes on the trail, or when I’m in my painting room, and it’s… clear, like he’s right behind me. But I don’t control it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I… mostly, yes. I don’t think I lose control of my body or anything.\n\nBut when I hear Garrett, it feels so real—like he’s right there behind me—that I can get caught up in it. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in the moment it’s hard to hold onto that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I feel like I am him. It’s more that I hear him—Garrett’s voice—like he’s standing behind me. On the trail, or in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens it feels… completely real.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that tends to happen right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Not really something specific… not that I can pin down. It just happens sometimes—on the trail, or when I’m in my painting room. Then I hear Garrett’s voice like he’s right behind me, clear as anything. In that moment it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you want to hear Garrett's voice when it happens?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, it’s Garrett, so there’s a part of me that… reacts to it. But I don’t want it to happen.\n\nIt’s more that it just does, and when it does, it’s so clear it feels like he’s right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett ever directly controls your actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I hear it, and it can shake me, but I don’t feel like it takes over my body or makes me do things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I’m still me, still in my body. It’s more just hearing him, not watching myself or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t like talking about it much. It’s Garrett’s voice, clear… like he’s right behind me. Even when I know he’s gone, in that moment it feels real, and it can make me feel like I’m doing something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett happen when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No… I can’t make it happen. It just comes on its own. Sometimes on the trail, or when I’m in my painting room, and it’s… clear, like he’s right behind me. But I don’t control it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I… mostly, yes. I don’t think I lose control of my body or anything.\n\nBut when I hear Garrett, it feels so real—like he’s right there behind me—that I can get caught up in it. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in the moment it’s hard to hold onto that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I feel like I am him. It’s more that I hear him—Garrett’s voice—like he’s standing behind me. On the trail, or in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens it feels… completely real.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that tends to happen right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Not really something specific… not that I can pin down. It just happens sometimes—on the trail, or when I’m in my painting room. Then I hear Garrett’s voice like he’s right behind me, clear as anything. In that moment it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you want to hear Garrett's voice when it happens?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, it’s Garrett, so there’s a part of me that… reacts to it. But I don’t want it to happen.\n\nIt’s more that it just does, and when it does, it’s so clear it feels like he’s right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett ever directly controls your actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I hear it, and it can shake me, but I don’t feel like it takes over my body or makes me do things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I’m still me, still in my body. It’s more just hearing him, not watching myself or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is a part of you or something separate from you?\n\nPatient: It feels separate. Like… I know Garrett’s gone, part of me knows that, but when I hear him it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room. In that moment it doesn’t feel like it’s coming from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is something that you are producing or is it something that is happening to you?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s happening to me. I don’t feel like I’m making it, exactly. I know Garrett’s dead, but when I hear him it’s… clear, like he’s right there behind me or in the room.\n\nDoctor: Did Garrett's death happen in a way that was particularly traumatic or upsetting for you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. It was upsetting, yeah. But I don’t want to get into the details right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there's a part of you that's disconnected from the rest of you when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really think of it that way. It’s more like… I hear him, and I get pulled into it, but I don’t know if that means part of me is disconnected.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F44.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… I keep hearing someone’s voice. Garrett’s. It sounds like he’s right behind me sometimes—on a trail, or when I’m in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Is Garrett's voice you hear saying anything to you, or is it just a sound?\n\nPatient: It’s words. His voice. Not just noise.\n\nSometimes it’s like he’s right behind me, saying something clear enough that I turn around… even though I know he’s gone. In the moment, it doesn’t feel like a memory. It feels real.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: Not exactly… I mean, it’s his voice, clear, like he’s right behind me. On the trail, or in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens it feels real. I don’t really want to get into all of what he says.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice influence your actions or decisions in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe. When I hear it, it feels like he’s right there behind me, and I’ll stop what I’m doing or turn around. Part of me knows Garrett’s dead, but in that moment it feels real enough that I react.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett go away when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No. Not really.\n\nI can try to ignore it, or leave the room, but when it happens it’s just… there. Like Garrett’s standing behind me. It feels too real in the moment to just make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Is Garrett's voice ever critical of you or telling you that you're doing something wrong?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels that way, yeah. I don’t like talking about it much. It’s Garrett’s voice, clear… like he’s right behind me. Even when I know he’s gone, in that moment it feels real, and it can make me feel like I’m doing something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett happen when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No… I can’t make it happen. It just comes on its own. Sometimes on the trail, or when I’m in my painting room, and it’s… clear, like he’s right behind me. But I don’t control it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I… mostly, yes. I don’t think I lose control of my body or anything.\n\nBut when I hear Garrett, it feels so real—like he’s right there behind me—that I can get caught up in it. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in the moment it’s hard to hold onto that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I feel like I am him. It’s more that I hear him—Garrett’s voice—like he’s standing behind me. On the trail, or in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens it feels… completely real.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that tends to happen right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Not really something specific… not that I can pin down. It just happens sometimes—on the trail, or when I’m in my painting room. Then I hear Garrett’s voice like he’s right behind me, clear as anything. In that moment it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you want to hear Garrett's voice when it happens?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, it’s Garrett, so there’s a part of me that… reacts to it. But I don’t want it to happen.\n\nIt’s more that it just does, and when it does, it’s so clear it feels like he’s right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett ever directly controls your actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I hear it, and it can shake me, but I don’t feel like it takes over my body or makes me do things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I’m still me, still in my body. It’s more just hearing him, not watching myself or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is a part of you or something separate from you?\n\nPatient: It feels separate. Like… I know Garrett’s gone, part of me knows that, but when I hear him it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room. In that moment it doesn’t feel like it’s coming from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is something that you are producing or is it something that is happening to you?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s happening to me. I don’t feel like I’m making it, exactly. I know Garrett’s dead, but when I hear him it’s… clear, like he’s right there behind me or in the room.\n\nDoctor: Did Garrett's death happen in a way that was particularly traumatic or upsetting for you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. It was upsetting, yeah. But I don’t want to get into the details right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there's a part of you that's disconnected from the rest of you when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… I keep hearing someone’s voice. Garrett’s. It sounds like he’s right behind me sometimes—on a trail, or when I’m in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Is Garrett's voice you hear saying anything to you, or is it just a sound?\n\nPatient: It’s words. His voice. Not just noise.\n\nSometimes it’s like he’s right behind me, saying something clear enough that I turn around… even though I know he’s gone. In the moment, it doesn’t feel like a memory. It feels real.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: Not exactly… I mean, it’s his voice, clear, like he’s right behind me. On the trail, or in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens it feels real. I don’t really want to get into all of what he says.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice influence your actions or decisions in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe. When I hear it, it feels like he’s right there behind me, and I’ll stop what I’m doing or turn around. Part of me knows Garrett’s dead, but in that moment it feels real enough that I react.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett go away when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No. Not really.\n\nI can try to ignore it, or leave the room, but when it happens it’s just… there. Like Garrett’s standing behind me. It feels too real in the moment to just make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Is Garrett's voice ever critical of you or telling you that you're doing something wrong?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it feels that way, yeah. I don’t like talking about it much. It’s Garrett’s voice, clear… like he’s right behind me. Even when I know he’s gone, in that moment it feels real, and it can make me feel like I’m doing something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett happen when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No… I can’t make it happen. It just comes on its own. Sometimes on the trail, or when I’m in my painting room, and it’s… clear, like he’s right behind me. But I don’t control it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I… mostly, yes. I don’t think I lose control of my body or anything.\n\nBut when I hear Garrett, it feels so real—like he’s right there behind me—that I can get caught up in it. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in the moment it’s hard to hold onto that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I feel like I am him. It’s more that I hear him—Garrett’s voice—like he’s standing behind me. On the trail, or in my painting room. I know he’s dead, but when it happens it feels… completely real.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that tends to happen right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Not really something specific… not that I can pin down. It just happens sometimes—on the trail, or when I’m in my painting room. Then I hear Garrett’s voice like he’s right behind me, clear as anything. In that moment it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you want to hear Garrett's voice when it happens?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, it’s Garrett, so there’s a part of me that… reacts to it. But I don’t want it to happen.\n\nIt’s more that it just does, and when it does, it’s so clear it feels like he’s right there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett ever directly controls your actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I hear it, and it can shake me, but I don’t feel like it takes over my body or makes me do things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're observing yourself from outside your body when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… I’m still me, still in my body. It’s more just hearing him, not watching myself or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is a part of you or something separate from you?\n\nPatient: It feels separate. Like… I know Garrett’s gone, part of me knows that, but when I hear him it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me on the trail, or in my painting room. In that moment it doesn’t feel like it’s coming from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is something that you are producing or is it something that is happening to you?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s happening to me. I don’t feel like I’m making it, exactly. I know Garrett’s dead, but when I hear him it’s… clear, like he’s right there behind me or in the room.\n\nDoctor: Did Garrett's death happen in a way that was particularly traumatic or upsetting for you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. It was upsetting, yeah. But I don’t want to get into the details right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like there's a part of you that's disconnected from the rest of you when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really think of it that way. It’s more like… I hear him, and I get pulled into it, but I don’t know if that means part of me is disconnected.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can choose when to think about Garrett or is the voice something that comes into your mind without you deciding to think about him?\n\nPatient: It’s not really something I choose.
